# VEYRA v3.1 — Complete Tool-by-Tool Help & Operating Guide

This is the team-facing operating manual for every tool/integration in the VEYRA catalog. It is intentionally written so a new analyst can answer five questions before touching a tool: **What is it? Why do we use it? What do I click in VEYRA? What can I safely start from the terminal? What evidence should I expect?**

## Non-negotiable execution model

```text
UI → Sudo Security Arsenal → Tool → Approved Scope → Purpose → Approval → Managed Worker → Evidence → Review → Remediation → Verification
Terminal → Enrolled Worker → Tool --help/version → VEYRA-approved command profile → Evidence collector → Receipt
```

VEYRA does not expose an unrestricted browser shell. Commands in this document are **orientation/health-check starters** unless explicitly generated by the authorized worker profile. Active scanning, exploitation, credential auditing, wireless testing and other high-impact activity require explicit authorization and scope. Hack-back, destructive counter-intrusion, persistence, C2, evasion and unauthorized access are prohibited.

## How to teach a new team member

1. Start with the tool purpose and category.
2. Confirm the target is owned/authorized.
3. Select the appropriate VEYRA worker.
4. Review the generated job contract and approval.
5. Run the smallest useful test first.
6. Preserve evidence, version and timestamps.
7. Validate findings before escalating severity.
8. Remediate and rerun the same controlled check.

## Access levels

- **Viewer:** read-only results.
- **Analyst:** investigate and correlate.
- **Admin:** authorized security operations.
- **Sudo / privileged admin:** high-impact tooling; MFA, scope, approval and isolated worker required.

## Tool reference

### Network Discovery

#### 1. Nmap (`nmap`)

**What it does:** Asset/service discovery
**Why VEYRA uses it:** Asset/service discovery
**Access:** Admin  
**Worker:** `lab_or_approved_worker`  
**Source:** `kali-2026.3.2-or-aegisx-extension`

**Use from the VEYRA UI**

1. Open **Sudo Security Arsenal**.
2. Search for this tool and open its Help/Details panel.
3. Select an explicitly approved asset, application, AI system, network or evidence set.
4. Enter the assessment purpose and required approval.
5. Review the generated contract, scope and worker before execution.
6. Run the controlled job and review normalized evidence.
7. Create/confirm remediation and verification tasks.

**Terminal starting point**

`sudo -n nmap --help` — first-run capability check on the enrolled worker. Use only the VEYRA-generated approved command profile for active testing.

**Expected evidence**

- hosts
- ports/services
- timestamps
- scope

**Common mistakes**

- Using an asset outside the approved scope
- Treating tool output as proof without validation
- Ignoring evidence provenance or timestamps

**Next step:** Validate the result, map it to VEYRA risk/graph/ATT&CK or ATLAS where applicable, remediate, and verify.

**Terminal starting point**

`sudo -n slsa-ai --help` — first-run capability check on the enrolled worker. Use only the VEYRA-generated approved command profile for active testing.

**Expected evidence**

- asset/model/agent identifier
- test or inspection context
- policy decision
- trace ID
- artifact hash
- timestamp

**Common mistakes**

- Testing production secrets or unapproved models
- Treating one benchmark as proof of security
- Failing to preserve model/tool/config versions

**Next step:** Correlate findings with VEYRA AI Security, Security Graph, OWASP, NIST AI RMF and MITRE ATLAS controls.
